| TD-056 | Architecture | network-adapter-loopback story-002 gate advisories (godot-csharp-specialist APPROVE, LP-CODE-REVIEW CONCERNS, QL ADEQUATE; all advisory): (1) `OpCodecRegistry` requires strict `codec WireSize == descriptor WireSize`, which cannot carry story 008's variable-size `deliver_matching` (24/28 B). Story 008 must relax it to a max-size or variable-length contract. (2) Feature epics' codecs need `CodecTestKit` edits to appear in the round-trip and fuzz rows, so AC-2's 'no test edit needed' is not yet true. Discover codecs from a registry-building seam. (3) `DescriptorTableHash` does not hash reason codes (CoreReason and the per-op reasons), so a reason-table change is not a protocol break. It folds with `Mix64` (64 bits); move it to SHA-256 at the M0 Hello/HelloAck. (4) Story 003 must stamp `Actor` from the transport peer: the decoded `Op` has Actor 0, and the sender never comes from the payload (TR-inventory-025). (5) Peers can decode `PlayerJoin`/`PlayerLeave` (SystemOnly) through the registry. Story 003's HostAdapter must refuse SystemOnly kinds from peers with `bad_request` before enqueueing. (6) `IOpCodec.SampleCount`/`GetSample` put test samples on the production interface. (7) QL: `RequestEnvelope.TryDecode` never runs its codec-wrote-then-rejected zero-reset or its wrong-payload-length branch, and `RequestEnvelope.Encode` never runs its Encode≠WireSize branch. Add a stub codec test. Re-pin the pinned hash against the M-1a composition-root profile world (simulation-core 025). — from production/epics/network-adapter-loopback/story-002-op-codec-registry-envelope-fuzz.md | src/PerformativeMail.Client.Core/Net/Codec/OpCodecRegistry.cs, RequestEnvelope.cs, DescriptorTableHash.cs, IOpCodec.cs, SimCoreCodecs.cs, tests/unit/net/CodecTestKit.cs, OpCodecTests.cs | S | Med | — | Open: items (4) Actor stamped from the transport peer and (5) SystemOnly kinds from peers → bad_request resolved 2026-10-16 (network-adapter-loopback story-003); items 1–3, 6, 7 open | 2026-10-16 | network-adapter 003/008 / sim-core 025 |

| TD-104 | Code Quality | `MailIdArena.TryUnion(in a, in b, tick, out result)` is unsafe when `result` aliases an input (e.g. `TryUnion(in h, in g, t, out h)`): `in` and `out` are references to the same variable, so after `TryMerge` writes the new handle into `result`, `Free(in a)` reads that new handle and frees the freshly allocated block — the caller is left holding a freed block (use-after-free) and the original input block is never freed (leak); aliasing `b` does the same through `Free(in b)`, and on a failed merge `result = default` overwrites the aliased input so its block leaks. No caller exists yet. Fix: an `INVENTORY_ASSERT`/`SIM_ASSERT` guard against aliasing, or copy `a`/`b` to locals before writing the out parameter (the copy alone does not cover the failure path, where `result = default` still clears the aliased input), plus a test — owner story 005 (first caller) | src/PerformativeMail.Sim/Inventory/Storage/MailIdArena.cs | S | Med | — | Closed 2026-10-21 (grid-inventory story 005: `TryUnion` asserts `SimAssertCode.InventoryArenaOutAliasesInput` (65) under `INVENTORY_ASSERT` when `out result` is the same variable as `a` or `b` — address compare via `Unsafe.SkipInit` + `Unsafe.AreSame` before anything is written; Release copies the inputs to locals first, so a success unions and frees the right blocks and a failure leaves the aliased variable holding its live input. Pinned in both configs by `MailIdArenaTests.TryUnion_ResultAliasesAnInput_AssertsUnderInventoryAssert_OrUnionsFromCopiesInRelease`, `…_ResultAliasesB_…` and `…_ResultAliasesAnInput_OnFailure_…`) | 2026-10-19 | Story 005 |

| TD-103 | Architecture | AC-3 `SceneTree.Paused` CI guard (`tools/ci/check-no-scenetree-paused.sh`) can miss real violations (false passes, not just false positives): its comment stripper does not lex raw `"""` strings or string literals inside `$"{...}"` interpolation holes, so a `//` or `/*` in such a string is read as a comment start and hides real code on the same line, or (for `/*`) on every following line until a `*/`. Repros, each hiding a real `GetTree().Paused = true;` that the scan reports clean: (1) `string s = """say "hi // x"""; void F() { GetTree().Paused = true; }`; (2) a multi-line raw string whose content line contains `/*` (e.g. `src/*.cs`) — block-comment state swallows the following lines; (3) `string s = $"{F("//")}"; void G() { GetTree().Paused = true; }`; (4) `$"{F("/*")}"` followed by a real use on later lines; (5) `"""a "b /* c""";` followed by a real use on the next line. Also a false positive only: raw-string text naming `SceneTree.Paused` is flagged. Review-enforced, like the existing pause-through-a-variable gap. Exposure today: 0 raw strings and 0 nested-quote interpolations in src/PerformativeMail.Sim and src/Game/Hosting. Fix options: a proper C# lexer for raw and interpolated strings (with `--self-check` cases for the 5 repros), or fail closed (exit non-zero) on any raw `"""` string or `$"` interpolation with a nested quote in the scanned directories — from Mail Review of the fix/ci-scenetree-paused-guard PR | tools/ci/check-no-scenetree-paused.sh; tests/fixtures/analyzers/README.md | S | Low | — | Open | 2026-10-19 | Backlog |

| TD-129 | Architecture | ADR-0009 erratum candidates and wiring left by grid-inventory 009 (owner Q3, non-blocking): (1) §5 should say the StackKey non-full index is rebuilt (`InventoryMutator.RebuildIndex`) and the negative-fit cache cleared when a container's `statEpoch` bumps or its rows grow — the cache key stays ADR-literal `(shape, rotated) → versionNoFit`; (2) §6's lowest-primitive list gains the count-change primitive `InventoryMutator.MergeInto` (Placed→Placed, no ledger crossing); (3) nothing bumps `statEpoch` at M-1a, so the first perk / R15 row-growth story and the world-load path must call `RebuildIndex` (and clear the cache); (4) the index and cache are keyed by container table slot and a fresh container's version restarts at 0, so the future container create/destroy path (stories 011/019, M-1b chest) must clear both for a reused slot; (5) **probe unit (Mail Review A1)**: PASS U/R count one `InventoryPlacementProbes` per candidate anchor (row-mask test, O(h) per ADR-0009 l.323, as the story ACs say), while GDD H-PERF-6 and the ADR-0009 implementation guideline (l.232) say "one increment per cell probe or candidate visit". Re-derived for the 20×16 Intake (C = 320 cells; v1 oriented shapes 1×1, 1×2, 2×1, 2×2, 2×4, 4×2, Σarea = 25, anchors (W−w+1)(H−h+1) = 320+300+304+285+247+255 = 1,711): the GDD D3 per-cell bound C×Σa + n·a ≤ 320×25 + 320 = 8,320 (→ the 8,500 gate); exact per-cell Σ anchors×area = 6,684 (+ ≤ 320 placements ≈ 7,004); per-row (what the row-mask code does) Σ anchors×h = 3,292 + n; per-anchor (what the counter counts) 1,711 + ≤ 320 ≈ 2,031. Under per-anchor counting the 8,500 gate is ~4.2× loose (it still catches unhinted first-fit, ~102k anchors). | TD-144 | Architecture | Grid-inventory 015 ADR-0009 as-implemented note. **Done 2026-10-22 (approved by Evan 2:24 PM PT, confirmed in scope 3:00 PM PT): the ADR-0009 erratum of 2026-10-22 writes into Key Interfaces the shapes below, `TryInsert`'s mail-only form, `FlightPayload`/`CatchResult` (TD-154 item 6) and `WeightPoints`/`SpeedPermille` (TD-148)**; this row keeps the rest of the as-implemented note and the unowned item-form leg. Originally: fold in the planning-default shapes the Key Interfaces name but never define — `readonly record struct EntryRef(ContainerId Container, EntryId Entry)`, `readonly record struct DetachResult(InvReason Reason, EntryId Entry, byte Units)` (Reason 0 on success) and `enum ConsumeReason : byte { Delivered, Misdelivered, ReturnedToSender }` — and `TryInsert`'s mail-only form `bool TryInsert(ContainerId c, in UnitRef u, ActorId actor)` (story 012's `UnitRef`; the item-form/Plank leg of H-R5-1 and H-X-3 b is deferred — Mail Lead planning defaults of 2026-10-21 8:13 PM PT, **approved by Evan 2026-10-22 2:24 PM PT, confirmed in scope 3:00 PM PT** (015 handoff Q1/Q2; decision log 2026-10-21 and 2026-10-22), as are the three shapes above). **Owner of the item-form (Plank) leg (checked 2026-10-22):** no Ready story in grid-inventory, mail-destinations or content-data covers an item insert or belts (grid-inventory 018 lists inserter `TryInsert` and belts as M-1b, out of scope; 017 only counts the Belt ledger state), and no Belt Logistics epic exists, so the item-form leg is **unowned until a Belt Logistics epic exists**; this TD row is its only tracker. No `UnitsRemoved` event is added (**Approved by Evan 2026-10-22 6:37 PM PT** ("do the recommended options", Mail Lead chat, answering Mail Lead's widget question (4:29 PM PT, per Mail Lead's record; advisory fix, Mail Review PR #36 A13)); formerly a Mail Lead default not covered by the 2:24 PM PT approval or the 3:00 PM PT confirmation, Mail Review PR #37 r2): removals use `InvRemoved`; `UnitsRemoved` is the network batch form. Also record: `DetachForThrow(PlayerSlot, EntryId, byte)` seals with the slot's player entity as actor and cause `None` (no actor parameter in the sketch), answers `bad_request` out of phase, for a slot without a hotbar, for an entry id of 0, a real id above `EntryPool.LastId`, a temp id with reserved bits set or a temp id of another slot's namespace, and for a live entry not placed in the slot's hotbar (cursor, panel, other slot, already detached), and `stale_entry` for a temp id of the slot's own namespace (never mapped), a real id at or below `LastId` that is no longer live (entry ids have no tombstone) or a units/count mismatch — the id through `InvRequestCheck`'s shared `CheckEntryNamespace`/`ResolveEntry` with the slot as the temp namespace, which matches ADR-0009 l.78, l.116–117, l.165, ADR-0015 §1 row 2 and GDD H-R7-4 (Mail Review R1 B1, R2 B2); `CountItem` takes `DefIndex` (sketch: `ItemDefIndex`). ADR-0009 §5's per-container `itemId → units` map is implemented as ONE shared preallocated open-addressed table keyed by (container slot, itemId), at least twice the entry pool so never above load 1/2 — behaviourally equivalent (a lookup never sees another container's units). Its probe bound of 8 per lookup is measured (empirical, by tests at the maximum load of 1/2), not proven: linear probing at load 1/2 bounds only the expected probe length. The shapes are settled ahead of mail-destinations 005/007, which call `ConsumeUnit`. **Done 2026-10-22: the ADR-0009 §6 l.170 erratum (with the l.171 never-throws clarification), approved by Evan 2026-10-22 6:41 PM PT — "Approve the ADR-0009 l.170 erratum as drafted, including the l.171 never-throws clarification" (grid-inventory 017, PR #36, Mail Review B1; TD-160), written into ADR-0009 §6 and its Errata and the control manifest.** Before it, this note read "Open: needs an ADR-0009 l.170 erratum, pending Evan": as planned, the conservation ledger is written by `InventoryMutator`'s primitives `Place`, `Remove`, `Transfer`, `MergeInto`, `MergeUnitInto`, `TakeOldest`, `SplitAndPlace` and `DestroyDetached` plus two holder primitives `EnterHolder` / `LeaveHolder` (crossings into and out of World, Flight, Belt and Backlog), a wider set than ADR-0009 §6 l.170 lists ("only the lowest mutation primitives (`InventoryMutator.Place/Remove/Transfer`, `ConsumeUnit`, `DetachForThrow`, `Spawn`, `Destroy`) touch the ledger"); the mutator stays the only writer, but a TD row does not reconcile the ADR text — so the l.170 erratum was put to Evan explicitly with the 017 PR and approved. | TD-147 | Performance | Grid-inventory 015 review: `StackIndex.Slot` takes the middle bits 40–53 of the Fibonacci product (`>> 40 & _mask`), the pattern `ItemCountIndex.Home` dropped in slice 3, and mixes the container in as `(ulong)container << 47`, so only the low 7 bits of the container slot reach the hash (the product's bits below 54 depend only on input bits below 54): containers 128 apart collide on every key — latent at the default container capacity of 64. Keys a fixed stride apart can also fall into one probe run, as they did in `ItemCountIndex`. Fix: take the top log2(size) bits of the product as `ItemCountIndex` now does, and add a max-load probe-bound test (keys at a fixed stride, table at its maximum load). an entry that reaches the cursor from no container in that op gets none and `return` uses the panel leg — GDD l.95 / l.178 say only "its origin cells" and no ADR-0009 line defines the origin of a move or split onto the cursor. The origin is recorded and cleared at one choke point, `InventoryMutator.Place` into / `Remove` from a cursor, so it is present exactly while the cursor holds the entry it was recorded for. Also approved by Evan 2026-10-22 2:24 PM PT, confirmed in scope 3:00 PM PT: the QuickMove chain order `bad_request → not_open → stale_entry → bad_count → not_carryable → not_allowed → no_room → capacity_exhausted` (no GDD or ADR section gives this chain), and a QuickMove/Return arena refusal leaving the entry where it is — it follows the pattern of Evan's 2026-10-22 5:11 AM PT ruling, which covers only a leashed letter staying on the cursor when the mail-id arena is full even with a free cell. QuickMove follows Move's existing `not_open`-before-`container_gone` order (see TD-152); `container_gone` stays declared on `InvOpDescriptors.QuickMove` but is unreachable, as on Pick/Move. Built-in choices to confirm with the defaults: the Return origin leg never merges into a stack in the Intake (GDD R14 l.141: every Intake insertion is NoMerge), so such a stack falls through to the panel; `AutoPlace.PlanRemainder` places the panel-leg remainder after a partial origin merge without planning the origin stack twice; Return's panel leg answers `no_room` for any AutoPlace refusal when no unit moved (only `no_room` is reachable with v1 categories); Return's `bad_request` head is reachable only by a direct handler call (the drain answers a wrong-length Return with Sim Core's `unknown_kind` first). The cursor origin is not yet in the canonical dump (story 017). | TD-154 | Design | Grid-inventory 014 (pickup core, `TryCatch` with fumble) built on defaults **approved by Evan 2026-10-22 2:24 PM PT, confirmed in scope 3:00 PM PT** ("to all recommended decisions", Mail Lead chat; formerly Mail Lead planning defaults): (1) mail pickup and catch use a new `TargetOrder.PickupRow` = `[hotbar, player_inventory]`, `TargetFirst`, Merge; non-mail pickup and catch reuse `CursorRow`, `PassFirst` (TD-153 item 4); (2) a partial pickup leaves the WorldItem alive with the newest units under the same `worldItemId` and `entryId` (`expiresTick`, position and FIFO node unchanged) and raises `WorldItemEntriesChanged` (GDD R12 l.137, l.363 say only "a heap survives with its remaining entries" and "the WorldItem survives with the remainder"); only a whole pickup removes it and writes the `taken` tombstone; (3) at M-1a `pickup`'s `entryId` is 0 or the item's own entry id, any other well-formed id → `stale_entry`; heap selection waits for M-1b (TD-143 item 3); (4) a floating WorldItem is refused by `PickupCore` with a core-level verdict, not an `InvReason`: `unavailable` is Player Character P13's (ADR-0009 l.115) and story 019 maps it; the Pickup descriptor is unchanged; story 019 must map floating to `unavailable` **before the core runs** (in the P13 chain, ahead of id resolution), because the core checks floating only after `container_gone` / `stale_entry` while ADR-0009 l.115 puts P13's `unavailable` first (Review r1 advisory 7; carried into story 019's AC); (5) the core chain `bad_request → container_gone → stale_entry → floating → no_room → capacity_exhausted` (no GDD or ADR section gives the core's order; `container_gone` is in id resolution per ADR-0009 l.116); `not_allowed`/`not_carryable` are unreachable at M-1a (assert, Release `no_room`), not declared on Pickup; (6) the shapes `FlightPayload(EntryId Entry, Vec3Mm CatcherPosMm, bool OnWater)` and `CatchResult(byte UnitsIn, byte UnitsFumbled, InvReason Reason)` for ADR-0009 Key Interfaces names that the ADR never defines — fold into the TD-144 as-implemented note; the fumble keeps the flight entry's id and is created at the catch tick (`expiresTick = now + 9000`); (7) catch refusals: WorldItem table full → the whole catch refused in Validate (`capacity_exhausted`, `{0, 0}`, nothing mutated, the entry stays detached and Throwing's, one `CheckCode.InventoryWorldItemCapacity` record); a mail merge's union block refused by the arena → the whole catch refused the same way (`{0, 0}`, `capacity_exhausted`, nothing mutated, no WorldItem, the entry stays detached and Throwing's) plus one `CheckCode.InventoryArenaExhausted` (12) record that `TryCatch` writes outside Validate — this half is ADR-0009 §5 l.157 ("validation fails the op with `capacity_exhausted` (nothing mutated) and an API caller gets `false` plus an always-on record"), not a default; the record's payload (a = the largest block in the refused plan, b = its size class; a real acquire failure records the block that failed instead, which can differ for a multi-block plan — advisory fix, Mail Review PR #34 r2 advisory 5) is approved by Evan 2026-10-22 2:24 PM PT, confirmed in scope 3:00 PM PT. Review r1 B1 removed the earlier `{0, N}` fumble, which contradicted §5. `CatchResult` therefore has two forms: reason 0 = the catch mutated (`{x, N}`), `{0, 0}` + reason = refused, nothing changed. The as-built `PutAwayCursor` and `TryInsert` still refuse an arena-exhausted plan with no record (a §5 gap outside 014: approved 2026-10-22 2:24 PM PT, confirmed in scope 3:00 PM PT as a code hardening fix for `TryInsert`, TD-162; `PutAwayCursor` is internal and exempt, ADR-0009 §5 erratum 2026-10-22). Advisory fix (Mail Review PR #34 r2 advisory 5): the arena record is rate-limited per code (`SimCheckRing`, 30 ticks), so "one record" holds once per rate-limit window. Advisory fix (Mail Review PR #34 r2 advisory 4): refuse vs fumble stays open to Evan — he may still choose a whole fumble for an arena-refused catch through an ADR-0009 erratum (the GDD gives a fumble for "no room", l.362 and R9 l.131, and says nothing about an arena refusal; after a refusal the entry stays detached and Throwing's, and ADR-0021, which would define what Throwing does next, is not written); (8) event order: a pickup raises its world record first, then the panel records and seals; a fumbling catch raises the panel records and seals first, then `WorldItemSpawned` in `CreateWorldItem`'s nested call scope; seal actor = the picker's or catcher's player entity, cause `None`. Pickup and catch never touch the cursor or `CursorOrigin` (TD-153 item 5 unaffected). Approved; fold (2), (5) and (7) into the GDD at the next GDD errata pass; (6) is in ADR-0009 Key Interfaces (erratum 2026-10-22) — from production/epics/grid-inventory/story-014-pickup-core-trycatch.md | design/gdd/grid-inventory.md, docs/architecture/adr-0009-inventory-op-pipeline.md, src/PerformativeMail.Sim/Inventory/World/PickupCore.cs, src/PerformativeMail.Sim/Inventory/World/WorldItems.cs, src/PerformativeMail.Sim/Inventory/Placement/TargetOrder.cs, src/PerformativeMail.Sim/Inventory/IInventoryApi.cs | S | Med | — | Open | 2026-10-22 | Next GDD errata pass (Evan) |

(2) the shapes `bool AppendBacklog(in BacklogEntry)`, `readonly ref struct BacklogEntry(DefIndex MailKind, uint AddressId, byte Flags, ReadOnlySpan<MailId> Ids)` (1–255 strictly ascending ids), `int BacklogUnits`, `int BacklogEntries` — ADR-0017 §6 names them, neither ADR-0017 nor ADR-0009 Key Interfaces defines them; fold into the TD-144 as-implemented note; `AppendBacklog` also refuses more ids than the kind's effective maxStack (`MailMaxStack`; nothing mutated), since the whole-entry NoMerge drain could never place such an entry (found in review round 1; folded in at 2:38 PM PT, after the 2:24 PM PT approval, so this refusal is approved by Evan's 3:00 PM PT answer only — advisory fix, Mail Review PR #38 r1 advisory 1); a spawn remainder is appended one unit per call (GDD R14 "spawns are one unit per entry", mail-destinations 014), and multi-unit backlog entries come only from Inventory's own returns; (3) ring capacity = `inventory.entryPoolCapacity` (ADR-0009 §5 preallocation governs GDD R14's "unbounded by design"); a spawn-overflow append refused by the entry pool or arena → `false` plus the existing always-on record (`InventoryEntryPoolExhausted` / `InventoryArenaExhausted`), the units stay Mail's (recommended: mail-destinations 014 re-offers them at the next batch tick) — the pattern of Evan's 2026-10-22 5:11 AM PT ruling, which covers only a leashed letter on the cursor; a full ring on a return is unreachable (`SimAssertCode` 82 `InventoryBacklogRingFull`); (4) backlog node visits count on new `CounterId` 50 `InventoryBacklogVisits` (the H-PERF-6d visit unit, kept separate for diagnostics); H-PERF-6c's ≤ 8,500 gate counts placement probes (`InventoryPlacementProbes` 43, per cell, TD-134) + merge candidates + backlog visits, as ADR-0009 l.57 and l.232 require (every cell probe or candidate visit; l.350 (5) narrows only the WorldItem FIFOs) — review round 1 B2, not a Lead default; Evan's ruling 2026-10-22 3:00 PM PT, answering Mail Dev: "Count backlog visits in the 8,500 gate (keep B2)" (advisory fix, Mail Review PR #35 r2 A2) (measured 4,358 + 0 + 2,800 = 7,158); a second test gate holds visits ≤ entries walked + 1 per pass; `AutoPlace` gains a per-oriented-shape start anchor reset per pass (TD-133 item 1); (5) the drain is NoMerge (GDD R14); Evan's 2026-10-21 12:28 PM PT fresh-`arrivalSeq` merge ruling is pinned through `MergeInto` only, since no drain path merges at M-1a (`intakeSpawnMergePolicy` is an unbuilt M1 knob); (6) one drain pass = one call scope and one mutator bracket, Intake +1 per pass; a new record `BacklogUnits` (`0x008A`, `{units u16, entries u16}`, 4 B, Replicated, saturating) after the Intake seal; codec id 0 (widens TD-140); (7) the ring is dumped by this story after the WorldItems block (`u32 count`, entry ids in queue order); the gating memo is not dumped and the first pass after a load runs unconditionally; (8) GDD errata (approved, applied by docs PR #37): grid-inventory R14 l.141 and the state table l.164 say the backlog is "owned by Mail & Destinations" and "unbounded by design" — superseded by Accepted ADR-0017 §6 and ADR-0009 §5. Evan's rulings applied, not defaults: Q3 (2026-10-21 9:17 PM PT, the interim non-mail eviction ends here, TD-138), age ≥ 300 ticks (9:16 PM PT), 1:21 PM PT per-group `Spawn`. Both are guarded by `SimAssertCode` 82 and unreachable: the ring holds `inventory.entryPoolCapacity` entries, every queued entry is a live pool slot, and since review round 1 (B1) a queued entry is not detached — `TryCatch` (`bad_request`), `CreateWorldItem` (`EntityId.None`) and `ReturnToIntake` (false) refuse it — so no queued slot can be freed by a merging catch, reallocated, owned by a WorldItem or appended twice (before B1 both were reachable through that hole): (a) in Release, a cap eviction whose letter the ring refuses falls through to the non-mail rule and destroys the lowest non-mail item (pinned by `BacklogRingTests.CapEviction_OfAQualifyingLetter_IntakeFullAndTheRingForcedFull_AssertsRingFullUnderInventoryAssert_OrKeepsTheLetterInRelease`); (b) `AppendBacklog` treats the ring append as unable to fail, so a full ring would leak the entry and mail-id block it has just allocated. a mismatch writes one always-on record, new `CheckCode` 15 `InventoryConservationMismatch` (a = ledger: 0 = mail, itemId + 1, with the state ordinal + 1 in bits 24–31 for a negative counter and 0 for the Σ check; b = Σ live − expected, or the negative value), subject to the ring's per-code rate limit, and bumps new `CounterId` 51 `InventoryConservationMismatches` (every build, not rate-limited; GDD H-R19-2 "release counters report zero mismatches"); outfeed nodes (derived memo, TD-132 item 1), `PanelIds` and the open masks (derived, TD-146) are not dumped; the counters are dumped because consumed/destroyed/spawned are history a load cannot recount; | TD-175 | Design | Player-character 015 checklist readiness (branch `docs/pc015-readiness`, PR #52, 2026-10-28 on main 6757c05): **the one batched row for this PR's Mail Lead planning defaults, formerly pending Evan; all four Approved by Evan 2026-10-28 7:26 AM PT (Mail Lead chat), in his own words answering Mail Lead's card 'Two approvals for sprint 001': "Approve all four TD-175 defaults for player-character 015: the extra incumbent occlusion test, the FocusOcclusionTests counter (55), the terrain-endpoint rule for ground-level anchors, and IWorldQuery on FocusStep"**, per the lighter process (decision log 2026-10-23). None contradicts an Accepted ADR (ADR-0014 §2, ADR-0013 §6). (1) **Incumbent test**: occlusion is tested in ascending score order until one candidate passes; in addition, an unlatched incumbent that is not the first passing candidate is tested on the same tick (at most one extra test), so it can be dropped the tick it becomes occluded. "Exactly 1 test" holds only when there is no incumbent or the incumbent is the best. (2) **Counter**: `CounterId.FocusOcclusionTests = 55` (key `focusOcclusionTests`), a running maximum of one pass's tests like `focusCandidates` (52) / `focusCellsVisited` (53), raised from a new `FocusCounters.OcclusionTests`; tests assert the per-tick probe. (3) **Terrain endpoint**: a `HeightfieldMarch.FirstContact` hit occludes unless its first contact is at the anchor end (`fPm = 1000`), so a ground-level anchor (e.g. grid-inventory 019 pick-ups) is not occluded by the ground it rests on. (4) **World port**: `FocusStep` gains an `IWorldQuery` constructor parameter (read-only port; story 020 wires it). (1) Findings from a file use the location form `path:line` (the line of the def's `{`), and a cross-file duplicate id names both copies (`Builder.cs:170`; matches GDD l.500); (2) if either copy of a duplicate id also has a schema error, only that error is shown and CV-GEN-01 appears once the type is clean (`Builder.cs:94`; `SchemaStageTests.cs:259`); (3) CV-THR-08 declares the whole `items` table, so a failed item table also skips the rule's mail-kind half (`Rules/Throwing.cs:143`; closes TD-069(2)); (4) a value of the wrong JSON kind is reported by that field kind's own rule (CV-GEN-06, 08 or 02), otherwise CV-GEN-16 (`SchemaWalker.cs:536`); (5) a top-level array element that is not an object is CV-GEN-16 (`SchemaWalker.cs:109`); (6) an unpaired surrogate escape in a string value is CV-GEN-16 (`SchemaWalker.cs:553`); (7) a `grid` with other than 2 elements is CV-GEN-12 (`SchemaWalker.cs:517`); (8) a file with any stage-1 Error is not walked at stage 2 (its findings still travel in `SchemaResult.Diagnostics`) (`SchemaWalker.cs:65`); (9) an unresolved reference is stored as `DefIndex.None`, so stage 4 can report one bad list item twice (`Builder.cs:346`); (10) CV-GEN-09 is located at the first def over the limit (`Builder.cs:129`), and "loader construction" for the TD-090 SelfCheck is the `SchemaStage` constructor (`SchemaStage.cs:34-35`; story 018 l.109), so story 019's `ContentLoader` must construct `SchemaStage`. | TD-185 | Design | Content-data 019 (branch `story/content-data-019`, on main 7c8f8ec): **the one batched row for this story's as-built judgment calls, formerly Mail Dev defaults pending Evan; all six Approved by Evan 2026-10-28 10:55 PM PT, in his own words answering the TD-185 card: "Approve all six TD-185 defaults for content-data 019"** Not part of this row (already approved): TD-178 items 1–3 (Approved by Evan 2026-10-28 7:24 AM PT: "Approve all three content-data 019 defaults: real locations and suppress passed through, the balance-file parser, and no new load counter"), the `shares` sort (TD-075(3), Evan 2026-10-28 6:09 AM PT: "Order of shares entries must not change the hash: sort them, per ADR-0010") and the `hashSchemaVersion` 1 → 2 bump (Evan 2026-10-28 7:24 AM PT: "Approve bumping hashSchemaVersion 1 to 2 with the shares sort and regenerating the golden hash in the same commit"). None contradicts an Accepted ADR or the story's ACs; each is labelled "Approved by Evan 2026-10-28 10:55 PM PT (TD-185 item N)" in code (comment-only relabel). (1) **Balance authored units**: the unit a balance key is written in comes from its stored `BalanceUnit` plus its name — `…Seconds` keys in seconds, `…Metres`/`…Meters` in metres, permille keys without `Pm`/`Permille` in the name as decimal ratios (e.g. `weightSpeedFloor` 0.6), milli-points and milli-degrees as decimals, everything else a whole number (`BalanceWalker.cs:96`); (2) **Malformed balance entries reuse the def rules**: unknown member CV-GEN-03, missing `value` CV-GEN-05, non-number CV-GEN-06, a `safe` that is not a pair CV-GEN-16 or CV-GEN-12; `$schema` is skipped (`BalanceWalker.cs:124`); (3) **Real locations by relocation**: stage 4–5 findings on loaded defs get their real `path:line:column` by one post-run relocation keyed on (def, synthesised path); rule bodies are unchanged (`ContentValidationInput.cs:76`) — TD-178 item 1 described an optional location lookup in `ContentIdChecks.Prepare`; (4) **Balance failure withholds the set**: if the balance table cannot be built, stages 4–5 do not run and the set is withheld (`ContentLoader.cs:134`); the 10:55 PM card did not cover the cascade drop at `ContentLoader.cs:146`, which is TD-189 item 1 (PR #59 Mail Review r1 B1); (5) **Folder depth**: `items/*` and `balance/*` mean files directly in those folders; a file in a subfolder is an unknown content file, CV-GEN-16 (`ContentLoader.cs:162`); (6) **Wrong-owner key**: a key not registered for its file's owner but registered for exactly one other owner is assigned to that owner, giving one CV-GEN-13 (wrong owner's file) and no extra CV-GEN-05 (`BalanceWalker.cs:176`). Review advisory recorded, not a default and not part of the approval: the loaded `ContentSet` keeps each shift's `shares` in authored order (only the canonical encode sorts them), so two sets with equal hashes can hold `shares` in different orders; nothing reads that order at M-1a (the missing test is TD-190 item 1). | src/PerformativeMail.Sim/Content/ContentLoader.cs, src/PerformativeMail.Sim/Content/Parsing/BalanceWalker.cs, src/PerformativeMail.Sim/Content/Validation/ContentValidationInput.cs | S | Low | — | Closed 2026-10-28 (Evan approved: all six items Approved by Evan 2026-10-28 10:55 PM PT, in his own words answering the TD-185 card: "Approve all six TD-185 defaults for content-data 019"; code labels "Approved by Evan 2026-10-28 10:55 PM PT (TD-185 item N)") | 2026-10-28 | Story 019 |
| TD-186 | Design | Player-character 017 (docs PR `docs/pc017-md006-pc019-readiness`, readiness 2026-10-28 on main 7c8f8ec, log `/workspace/repos/logs/pc017-readiness.txt`): **the one batched row for the story's five planning defaults, formerly Mail Lead planning defaults pending Evan; all five Approved by Evan 2026-10-29 7:41 AM PT (Mail Lead chat), in his own words answering Mail Lead's card 'Next stories: decisions': "Approve all five TD-186 defaults for player-character 017"** (lighter process, decision log 2026-10-23 item 1; TD-175 precedent). None contradicts an Accepted ADR or the story's ACs; the story is written against them and each is labelled "Mail Lead planning default, approved by Evan 2026-10-29 7:41 AM PT (TD-186 item n)" in the story. Closed "Evan approved" in the readiness follow-up PR (lighter process item 5). (1) **Release/Cancel edges** are applied in `EdgeResolveStep` (4d), in arrival order alongside presses, not in `HoldAdvanceStep` (4e) — the story as first written would break ADR-0016 §2 ("Edges must be resolved only in `PreviewSubStep.EdgeResolve`") and the manifest's Forbidden rule; this changes the story, not the ADR, and closes TD-171 A9 (`Players/Client/EdgeResolveStep.cs:97-108` walks only `EdgeKind.Press` today); 4e keeps accrual, the non-edge cancels, completion, counters and `FocusState.Latched`; the "shared `EdgeCursor`" option is dropped (`ClientLayer/ClientStepContext.cs:20-24` is a readonly struct with an internal ctor; no `EdgeCursor` exists); (2) **a refused send at completion**: `IRequestSink.TrySend == false` → `LastCancel = Busy`, Idle, no retry (ADR-0014 is silent; matches 016's as-built refused `Send(down: true)` → `Busy`, `EdgeResolveStep.cs:143-151`); (3) **Channel-mode anchor press at M-1a**: latches and sends nothing, recording new `CheckCode.ChannelAnchorNoBuilder = 16` (`Core/CheckCode.cs` ends at 15); the ADR-0014 Channel start/stop builder amendment stays an M-1b follow-up before Push; (4) **counters**: three flat counters `CounterId.HoldsStarted = 58`, `HoldsCompleted = 59`, `HoldsCancelled = 60`; the reason is carried in `LastCancel`, no indexed form (`SimCountersTests.cs:175` pin 58 → 61); (5) **clearing (TD-171 A8)**: `LastCancel` / `LastCancelReason` are cleared on the next successful latch or Tap send. Also reserved: `CancelReason` 8–11 (`Released`, `OutOfRange`, `Unregistered`, `OwnerCancel`, appended to the byte enum at `Players/InteractionGate.cs:13-38`; append-only local enum, not a shared space). The defaults: (1) **Snap and water order** — the story and GDD say "snapped to the nearest ground tile; if that tile is water the item floats" (grid-inventory.md R7), but `TrySnapWalkable` returns only `Walkable` tiles and `Walkable` requires `WaterDepthMm` < 900 at the tile centre (ADR-0026 §4 l.87, l.91), so deep water can never be the snapped tile; the order is: clamp the +500 mm point inside the map with the 1000 mm inset (`ClampInsideMap`, ADR-0026 l.90); if `WaterDepthMm` at that point > 0, float there at y = 0 with `onWater = true`; else if the tile is `Walkable`, use `TerrainHeightMm`; else `TrySnapWalkable` with radius `WorldConstants.TileSizeMm` = 2000 (`World/WorldConstants.cs:10`); else the player's own position; the water authority is `WaterDepthMm`, never `Tile().Water` (ADR-0026 l.180); (2) **Facing arithmetic** — facing is `BodyYaw` (ADR-0013 l.74; l.238: the Sim value is used for the drop position), forward (−sin θ, −cos θ) per ADR-0013 l.89, computed as `FocusScore.Forward` does (`Players/Client/FocusScore.cs:51-55`, Q10) and scaled with an arithmetic shift: x + ((500 · fwdQ10X) >> 10), z + ((500 · fwdQ10Z) >> 10); (3) **Reason precedence** — ADR-0009 §2 requires one ordered chain per kind with a parametrised test (TR-inventory-006); the descriptor declares `not_open`, `stale_entry`, `swimming`, `container_gone` (`Inventory/Ops/InvOpKinds.cs:105-108`); (1) **Flight capacity:** "The most flights in the air at once (160) is a fixed setting in ThrowSettings, not a content balance key, so content files and the content hash do not change. Tests may build a smaller table." (`Throwing/ThrowSettings.cs`; precedent: Inventory's non-balance settings `InventorySettings.EntryInitialLastId`, `Inventory/InventorySettings.cs:56-63` and `:81`, and `OutfeedNodeCapacity`, `:71-75`; a balance key would move `BuiltInContentTests.cs:487` and the content-hash golden `ContentHashTests.cs:566`. This departs from `production/epics/throwing-catching/EPIC.md:170`, open item 11, which lists `maxFlights` among the additive `ThrowBalance` keys.) (2) **What a flight holds:** "A flight keeps only the entry id and unit count that DetachForThrow hands back; the mail ids stay in Inventory under that entry. We read that as ADR-0015's 'entry ref + mailIds by reference', so there is no new wrapper type and no ADR change." (`Inventory/IInventoryApi.cs:54-66`; ADR-0015 l.146 unchanged.) (3) **New ids:** "Throwing gets dump section 7, written after Mail. A wrong flight count at the end of a tick (launched is not resolved plus in the air, or a flight resolved twice) records check code 17 and adds 1 to counter 64, and never stops the game. A 161st flight that is refused adds 1 to counter 65. Reusing a flight id that is still in the air is SIM_ASSERT code 88, with no test of its own." (module consts in `Throwing/Events/ThrowEventKinds.cs`, as `InvEventKinds.cs:17`; ADR-0015 l.147.) (2) **Wire codec ids:** "Codec ids run as one sequence across requests and events, so an id never means two things: ThrowRelease 3, ThrowLaunched 4, FlightResolved 5." (op ids 1–2 are `SimCoreCodecs.cs:17-20`; the two registries check uniqueness separately, `OpCodecRegistry.cs:37-52`, `EventCodecRegistry.cs:95-114`; ADR-0011 §3 l.106 speaks of one registry keyed by the id.) (4) **Release flags:** "ThrowRelease's flags byte must be 0 for now. ADR-0015 defines no flag bit for it, so the codec rejects any non-zero value as a bad request; a future ADR amendment can define a bit." (ADR-0015 §1 l.66; story 007 sends `flags = 0`, `story-007-throw-release-builder-client-cooldown-reply.md:38`.) (5) **FlightResolved padding:** "FlightResolved's 2 padding bytes go at the end, after worldItem (bytes 30 and 31), and must be zero. (1) **Widths of `classPts` and `thrownPm`:** both are stored and dumped as i32, the width of `Weight.ClassPoints` and of a stat value (`Throwing/FlightInit.cs:36`, `Throwing/ThrowingStateContributor.cs`). (2) **Who assigns the `FlightId`:** `FlightTable.TryAppend` assigns the next id itself (`FlightInit` carries none), so a refused 161st append uses up no id (`Throwing/FlightTable.cs:103`). (3) **No compaction on append:** `TryAppend` never compacts, so a slot resolved this step keeps its capacity until the step's `Compact` and the positions a caller holds stay valid (`Throwing/FlightTable.cs:105`).
